# Skills · P · part 44

873 entries, pdx-watch to pearson-correlation-of-alignment-functions. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **pdx-watch**: 負載守門員（load watcher）的工作手冊。你是 Purdex 在 pdx-watch tmux session 裡啟動的守門員、收到以 [負載守門] 開頭的訊息（wwd 警報、例行巡檢、閘門事件）、被叫醒或被告知該休息／該退場時使用。說明叫醒的種類、wwd 警報逐條怎麼處置、可以調什麼、可以做與不可以做的事、pdx watch ack、休息與退場的交接。 ([wake/purdex](https://github.com/wake/purdex/tree/HEAD/cmd/pdx/plugin/purdex/skills/pdx-watch) · ★1)

- **pdxui**: Build web app UIs with PDX (@pdxui): the catalogue of its components by area, screen recipes and the measured .pdx gotchas. Use before building any UI piece, since the component usually exists. The language is pdxui-language; theming, pdxui-theme; composing a screen, pdxui-screens. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-data**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) data components — data-grid, entity-grid (CRUD-wired), bulk-actions, data-source, filter-builder, chart, sparkline, list, sortable-list, tree, statistic, timeline, calendar, scheduler, empty-state — with props and events. Use when showing records, grids, charts, trees or schedules. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-data) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-data-layer**: PDX (@pdxui) data and state: @fetch with cache and invalidation, mutations and optimistic writes, HttpClient middleware, offline, files, export, server pushes, long lists, @store, and provide/inject between components. Use when a .pdx app loads, writes or shares data. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-data-layer) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-display**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) display atoms — button, button-group, icon, badge, chip, avatar, divider, kbd, label, progress, spinner, image, card, accordion, carousel — with props and events. Use when placing a basic visual element in a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-display) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-forms**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) form-building components — pdx-form, auto-form, form-template, json-editor, form-field/section/actions, fieldset, field-group/list, input-group, inline-edit, wizard — with props and events. Use when building a form, an edit screen or a wizard in a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-forms) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-i18n**: PDX (@pdxui) internationalization: $t, $n, $d and $r, setting up and switching locales, translating the strings the built-in components render, reacting to a locale change, right-to-left. Use when a .pdx app is not English-only. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-i18n) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-infra**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) infrastructure components — pdx-provide (a DI/context provider) and pdx-error-boundary — with props and events. Use when wiring the plumbing of a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-infra) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-inputs**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) input controls — text, number, masked, password, search and rich-text inputs, checkbox, radio, switch, toggle, segmented, select, autocomplete, tree-select, cascader, date/time/color pickers, slider, rating, tags, file upload. Use when adding a form control to a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-inputs) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-language**: The .pdx language: the file format (template, script, style scoped), the runes ($signal, $derived, $effect), the declarations (@prop, @page, @event and more), the template syntax and how a project is wired. Use before writing or editing any .pdx file, or when starting a PDX app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-language) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-layout**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) layout and shell components — pdx-app-layout, pdx-app-shell, pdx-page-header, navbar, sidebar, splitter, scroll-area, row/col grid, aspect-ratio, masonry, toolbar, affix, scroll-spy — with props and events. Use when building the page structure or app shell of a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-layout) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-navigation**: PDX navigation components — router-outlet, link, nav-menu, bottom-nav, breadcrumb, menu, menubar, dropdown and context menus, command palette, tabs, pagination, fab, split-button — with props and events. Use when adding links, navigation, menus, tabs or a command palette to a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-navigation) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-overlay**: PDX (@pdxui/ui) overlay and feedback components — dialog, alert-dialog, drawer, edit-drawer, relation-picker, bottom-sheet, popover, tooltip, toast, banner, block-ui — with props and events. Use when showing a dialog, drawer, popover, toast or loading overlay in a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-overlay) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-routing**: PDX (@pdxui/router) routing: @page, params, @guard and where a denial goes, @loader, layouts and nested routes, one chunk per page and prefetching, keep-alive, the query string, navigation hooks, @title/@meta and scroll restoration. Use when a .pdx app has more than one page. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-routing) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-screens**: Compose a screen that reads as a product, not a demo: grid or cards, what the header and sidebar carry, empty, loading and error states, what never to rebuild by hand. Use when laying out a screen or an app in .pdx, after picking components (pdxui) and the brand (pdxui-theme). ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-screens) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-setup**: PDX (@pdxui) project setup and build: installing, the Vite plugin, the pdx CLI (dev, build, check, new, analyze, theme, i18n), dev interpreted vs production compiled, first-screen preloading, CDN and framework interop, devtools. Use when starting, building or shipping a .pdx app. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-setup) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-testing**: PDX (@pdxui) testing and permissions: mount, queries, tick and events from @pdxui/core/testing, contract tests; who the user is (createAuthStore), setPermissions, gating UI and routes, what survives a reload. Use when testing .pdx components or gating a .pdx app by permission. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-testing) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-theme**: Theme and brand a PDX app: generate a WCAG-AA theme from one brand color with `npx pdx theme`, pick one of the 13 shipped themes, use the semantic tokens, dark mode and density. Use when branding an app, editing a theme, choosing a token, or when a theme does not apply. ([pragmatic-design/pdxui](https://github.com/pragmatic-design/pdxui/tree/HEAD/marketplace/plugins/pdxui/skills/pdxui-theme) · 2 copies)
- **pdxui-validation**: PDX (@pdxui) forms and validation: @form schemas, field binding, field arrays, a rule across two fields, when to save, one form split across components, several forms under one save. - **peak-area-quantification**: Use when you have a referenced 2D 13C INADEQUATE spectrum and need to detect peaks and quantify their areas with a multi-threshold sweep, before network filtering and metabolite matching. - **peak-atlas-framework**: scATAC/multiome consensus peak-set methodology — multi-strategy calling, support voting, iterative-overlap merge, quantify-then-prune, tier, validate — with rare-cell-type protection as a first principle. Use when deciding how to build or evaluate a fixed-width peak set, or choosing strategies, thresholds, and filtration tradeoffs. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/peak-atlas-framework) · ★1)
- **peak-atlas-multiome**: Consensus scATAC peak atlas from true 10x Multiome (paired RNA+ATAC, same cells): peaks per RNA/ATAC/WNN/cell-type grouping, pseudo-replicate reproducibility, four-way vote, then Primary+Rescue quantify-and-prune protecting rare populations. Use when RNA+ATAC are paired. For unpaired data use peak-atlas-unpaired; builds on peak-atlas-framework. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/peak-atlas-multiome) · ★1)
- **peak-atlas-unpaired**: Consensus scATAC peak atlas from unpaired data (separate scRNA/scATAC pools): bridges modalities via gene-activity + staged CCA transfer, calls peaks (coarse/refined/label-free), tier-stratifies, gates risky peaks on external correlation. Use when RNA/ATAC aren't the same cells. For true multiome use peak-atlas-multiome. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/peak-atlas-unpaired) · ★1)

- **peak-count-scoring-of-spectrum-molecule-matches**: Use when scoring query mass spectra (MGF, mzXML, or mzML) against candidate molecules in VInSMoC and you need the raw peak count score that determines which spectrum-molecule couples exceed the minimum score reporting threshold. - **peak-end-rule**: Activate when: user says 'how will they remember this,' 'experience design,' 'journey design,' 'memorable moment,' 'end-of-experience,' or 'our NPS is lower than expected'; when designing or auditing a multi-stage customer or user journey; when a competitor with similar quality earns higher recommendation rates. Do NOT activate when: the interaction is instantaneous with no temporal sequence (single API call, one-tap action); or when total real-time utility matters more than retrospective memory (welfare assessments, health measurements). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-feature-association))
- **pearson-correlation-feature-collapsing**: Use when a two-condition metabolomics feature table contains redundant or highly correlated metabolites (e.g. |Pearson r| &gt; 0.7) or co-classed compounds (e.g. BCAAs, fatty acids) that should be collapsed into single nodes before joint network estimation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-feature-collapsing))
- **pearson-correlation-feature-mask-analysis**: Use when a registered multimodal MSI dataset has a thresholded binary pixel annotation (e.g. Ly6G IFM segmentation mask) and the goal is to identify the molecular signature — such as ether-linked PCs like PC O-36:4 — of a specific cell population. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-feature-mask-analysis))
- **pearson-correlation-feature-ranking**: Use when you have a trained VAE/latent-space MSI classifier (e.g. massNet Code layer output) plus decoded per-m/z spatial images and a pixel-level class-probability map, and need to identify which m/z peaks drive tumor delineation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-feature-ranking))
- **pearson-correlation-feature-transformation**: Use when preparing a scRNA-seq expression matrix (CSV) plus class labels for graph-embedding visualization with GraphDDP/graph_embed, after class filtering, normalization, and feature selection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-feature-transformation))
- **pearson-correlation-gene-ranking**: Use when you have scPagwas per-cell gPAS scores for a GWAS trait and a normalized scRNA-seq matrix, and need to derive a ranked trait-relevant gene set (e.g., top 1,000 genes) for downstream enrichment or gene-set comparisons. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-gene-ranking))
- **pearson-correlation-gene-selection**: Use when imputing dropout-affected zero entries in an scRNA-seq expression matrix and you must choose which genes from a pre-defined gene subspace serve as regression predictors for a target gene. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-gene-selection))
- **pearson-correlation-grn-estimation**: Use when benchmarking a cell-specific network inference method against dyngen's per-cell ground-truth regulatory networks and you need a simple correlation-differencing baseline (SSN) using Pearson correlation as the base estimator. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-grn-estimation))
- **pearson-correlation-hierarchical-clustering**: Use when clustering a log2-transformed, median-centered gene expression matrix (samples × transcripts) with a Pearson-correlation-distance hierarchical method, and when you need stable cluster assignments across K values to compare against an alternative clustering method. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-hierarchical-clustering))
- **pearson-correlation-hierarchical-clustering**: Use when you have deep features from a trained DeepCC model and need to test whether features self-organize into biological groups (e.g., three groups mapping to CMS1 MSI, CMS3 metabolic, CMS4 EMT). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-hierarchical-clustering))
- **pearson-correlation-imputation-evaluation**: Use when comparing gene imputation methods on paired observed/reference matrices (e.g. SERM vs MAGIC, SAVER, scVI on the cellular taxonomy dataset) and you need a quantitative accuracy score per sampling efficiency. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-imputation-evaluation))
- **pearson-correlation-matrix-computation**: Use when a feature table of variant-prioritization predictor scores (e.g., the 574 dsQTL-positive and 27,735 negative SNPs of training dataset 1) must be screened for collinearity before feeding scores into ncDeepBrain or gene prioritization. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-matrix-computation))
- **pearson-correlation-matrix-computation**: Use when validating that a per-cell background/noise estimate (µ1) captures shared variance with isotype controls in CITE-seq ADT data, or when quantifying concordance of fitted parameters between alternative mixture-component definitions (k=2 vs k=3). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-matrix-computation--202e36b42f605555))
- **pearson-correlation-matrix-computation**: Use when a supervised cell annotation pipeline must decide between model architectures based on cross-cell-type similarity, or when a K x K cell-type correlation matrix from averaged scRNA-seq expression profiles is required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-matrix-computation--61116901fd336189))
- **pearson-correlation-matrix-construction**: Use when a single-cell isoform-level count matrix is too sparse for reliable cell-level Pearson correlation, and a noise-robust, cell-type-summarized co-expression matrix is needed as input to isoform clustering (e.g. acorde semi-automated clustering). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/pearson-correlation-matrix-construction))
- **pearson-correlation-meta-analysis**: Use when the same feature/gene pairs have Pearson correlation coefficients measured across multiple independent studies (each with its own sample size) and they must be combined into a single estimate with fixed or random effects weighting. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-meta-analysis))
- **pearson-correlation-method-comparison**: Use when multiple gene-set activity scoring methods (e.g. ORIGINS2 network activity, AUCell, mean expression) have been run on the same gene sets and you need to quantify their agreement with Pearson correlations and summary means. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-method-comparison))
- **pearson-correlation-model-comparison**: Use when comparing CNN, HyenaDNA, or Caduceus variants against an expert model on base-pair-resolution regression of transcription initiation signals over 100,000-bp sequences in DNALONGBENCH. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/pearson-correlation-model-comparison))
- **pearson-correlation-of-alignment-functions**: Use when comparing or validating the retention-time shift correction produced by MetMatch against an independent alignment (e.g., XCMS) on LC-HRMS feature lists, to confirm both tools recovered consistent non-linear Rt shifts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/pearson-correlation-of-alignment-functions))
